    }
}

/// The prefix expressions of a scan as `IndexKeyExpr`s, for callers that
/// pin values rather than expressions.
pub fn pinned_literals(prefix: &[Value]) -> Vec<IndexKeyExpr> {
    prefix.iter().map(|value| IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(Expr::Literal(value.clone()))).collect()
}

/// Something that can answer an `exists`/`created` probe while a rule runs.
pub trait Probe {
    fn reference(&mut self, _table: &str, _id: &Value, _field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, crate::error::RuntimeError> { Ok(None) }
    /// Exact count-predicate transitions, distinct from ordinary witness expiry.
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { None }
    /// Exchange the private expiry accumulator; ordinary probes have none.
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, _expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { None }
    /// Coverage of the most recent leaf, consumed once by the shared evaluator.
    fn take_unknown(&mut self) -> bool { false }
    /// Called only when evaluation loads a field, after short-circuit decisions.
    fn field(&mut self, _field: &str, _old: bool) {}
    /// Apply the schema's point-key null rule before storage access. Timeline
    /// evaluation can visit operands that logical short-circuiting would skip.
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError>;
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool;
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum Verdict { Readable, Denied, #[default] Unknown }

impl Verdict {
    fn not(self) -> Self {
        match self { Self::Readable => Self::Denied, Self::Denied => Self::Readable, Self::Unknown => Self::Unknown }
    }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let decisive = if conjunction { Self::Denied } else { Self::Readable };
        if self == decisive || other == decisive { decisive }
        else if self == Self::Unknown || other == Self::Unknown { Self::Unknown }
        else { decisive.not() }
    }
}

/// Evaluate a rule over a row image (and its old image, for update rules).
pub fn evaluate(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    evaluate_verdict(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|verdict| verdict == Verdict::Readable)
}

pub(super) fn evaluate_verdict(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Verdict, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe);
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok(answer) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|answer| answer.value)
}

pub(super) fn earliest(a: Option<i64>, b: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> {
    match (a, b) { (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)), (a, b) => a.or(b) }
}

struct Timeline {
    value: Verdict,
    changes: Vec<(i64, Verdict)>,
    // Proven from rule structure and verdicts, never from witness deadlines.
    invariant: bool,
}
impl Timeline {
    fn constant(value: Verdict) -> Self { Self { value, changes: Vec::new(), invariant: true } }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let (mut left, mut right) = (self.value, other.value);
        let mut result = Self::constant(left.combine(right, conjunction));
        let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
        result.invariant = (self.invariant && left == decisive)
            || (other.invariant && right == decisive) || (self.invariant && other.invariant);
        let (mut a, mut b) = (self.changes.as_slice(), other.changes.as_slice());
        let mut previous = result.value;
        while let Some(time) = earliest(a.first().map(|(t, _)| *t), b.first().map(|(t, _)| *t)) {
            // All rows expiring at the same instant disappear together. This
            // preserves correlations, including A or not A and A and not A.
            if let Some((head, tail)) = a.split_first() && head.0 == time { left = head.1; a = tail; }
            if let Some((head, tail)) = b.split_first() && head.0 == time { right = head.1; b = tail; }
            let value = left.combine(right, conjunction);
            if value != previous { result.changes.push((time, value)); previous = value; }
        }
        result
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#11 — observe the existing evaluation, never re-probe for diagnostics.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(super) fn evaluate_update(rule: &RuleExpr, both: bool, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<(bool, &'static str), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = if both {
        let RuleExpr::And(terms) = rule else { unreachable!("old_and_next is a conjunction") };
        compound_timeline(terms, true, viewer, old, next, false, probe)
            .map(|(answer, failed)| (answer, if failed == Some(0) { "old image" } else { "next image" }))
    } else {
        timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, false, probe).map(|answer| (answer, "named transition or identical old/next images"))
    };
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok((answer, _)) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|(answer, image)| (answer.value == Verdict::Readable, image))
}

fn compound_timeline(terms: &[RuleExpr], conjunction: bool, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<(Timeline, Option<usize>), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
    let mut answer = Timeline::constant(decisive.not());
    let mut failed = None;
    for (index, term) in terms.iter().enumerate() {
        let part = timeline(term, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
        if failed.is_none() && part.value != Verdict::Readable { failed = Some(index); }
        answer = answer.combine(part, conjunction);
        // Preserve the original timeline traversal and its physical allowance.
        if answer.value == decisive && answer.invariant { break; }
    }
    Ok((answer, failed))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — next_expiry is the first expiry boundary that changes
// the complete bound rule's verdict, not a branch or first witness lifetime.
// Leaves prove their timeline within the original shared allowance. Combining
// those timelines performs no further storage reads.
fn timeline(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Timeline, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => {
            let conjunction = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::And(_));
            compound_timeline(terms, conjunction, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|(answer, _)| answer)
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let mut answer = timeline(inner, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
            answer.value = answer.value.not();
            for (_, value) in &mut answer.changes { *value = value.not(); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        _ => {
            probe.take_changes();
            let mut missing = false;
            let value = evaluate_leaf(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe, &mut missing)?;
            let expiry = probe.replace_expiry(None);
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(if probe.take_unknown() || missing { Verdict::Unknown } else if value { Verdict::Readable } else { Verdict::Denied });
            if let Some(changes) = probe.take_changes() {
                answer.changes = changes;
                answer.invariant = false;
                return Ok(answer);
            }
            // An absent witness stays absent as rows expire. Positive probes
            // (including created facts with extra tests) need their alternatives
            // in both lifetime worlds; the observed deadline cannot decide this.
            answer.invariant = rule_probes(rule) == 0 || answer.value == Verdict::Denied;
            if answer.value == Verdict::Readable && let Some(time) = expiry { answer.changes.push((time, Verdict::Denied)); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
    }
}

fn evaluate_leaf(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow => Ok(true),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Ok(false),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? == term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? != term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Compare(op,left,right) => {
            let left = term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let right = term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let (Value::Int(a),Value::Int(b)) = (left,right) else { return Ok(false); };
            Ok(match op { crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte => a >= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt => a > b,
                crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte => a <= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lt => a < b, _ => false })
        }
        RuleExpr::And(_) | RuleExpr::Or(_) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => unreachable!("compound rule"),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
            // Bind each operand once, through one codec/allocation path for both leaves.
            let created = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Created { .. });
            if created && !created_allowed {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Schema("created probes are valid only in insert rules".into()));
            }
            let key = key.iter().map(|term| self::term(term, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            if created {
                if !probe.created(table, index, &key) { return Ok(false); }
                if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
            }
            let equalities = equalities.iter().map(|(column, value)| Ok((column.clone(), term(value, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?))).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, crate::error::RuntimeError>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            probe.exists(table, index, &key, &equalities)
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn term(term: &RuleTerm, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    Ok(match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => { probe.field(field, false); next.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) => { probe.field(field, true); old.and_then(|old| old.field(field).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::Reference { column, old: previous, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, old: previous, .. } => {
            probe.field(column, *previous);
            let row = if *previous { old } else { Some(next) };
            let mut value = row.and_then(|r| r.field(column).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null);
            for (table, field) in term.references() {
                match probe.reference(table, &value, field)? {
                    Some(next) => value = next,
                    None => { *missing = true; return Ok(Value::Null); }
                }
            }
            value
        },
        RuleTerm::Viewer => Value::Id(viewer.to_owned()),
        RuleTerm::Literal(value) => value.clone(),
    })
}

//! Lifetime-bound rule inputs, distinct from historical recorded attribution.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — captures have their parent's type and activation lifecycle.
use super::*;

pub const PARENT_DEADLINE: &str = "$parentDeadline";
pub const DEADLINE: &str = "$deadline";
pub const CHILDREN: &str = "$children";
pub const LIVE_CHILDREN: &str = "$liveChildren";

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Through {
    /// Bounded live maintenance; absence preserves captured wire bytes.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub live: Option<u32>,
    pub reference: String,
    pub parent: String,
    /// Child engine field -> immediate parent field (including its captures).
    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub expiring: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn engine_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        (self.ephemeral.is_some() && field == DEADLINE) || self.recorded.contains_key(field)
            || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
                t.fields.contains_key(field) || matches!(field, DEADLINE | PARENT_DEADLINE)
            })
    }
    /// One classification for every expiry gate, including inherited deadlines.
    pub fn is_expiring(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.expire.is_some() || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)
    }
    pub fn expiry_column(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        if self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.through.is_some() && self.is_expiring() {
            Some(DEADLINE)
        } else {
            self.expire.as_deref()
        }
    }
    pub fn deadline(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        match self
            .expiry_column()
            .and_then(|field| row_image(row, self).field(field).ok().cloned())
        {
            Some(Value::Int(at)) => Some(at),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    /// Never trust a restored effective instant; recompute from immutable capture.
    pub fn recompute_deadline(&self, row: &mut Value) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.through.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let authored = self
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|c| match row_image(row, self).field(c) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                _ => None,
            });
        let parent = match row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE)? {
            Value::Int(at) => Some(*at),
            Value::Null => None,
            _ => {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Input(
                    "captured parent deadline must be a time or null".into(),
                ));
            }
        };
        let deadline = authored
            .into_iter()
            .chain(parent)
            .min()
            .map(Value::Int)
            .unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = row {
            fields.insert(DEADLINE.into(), deadline);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn map_fields(&self, map: &impl Fn(&str) -> String) -> Self {
        let term = |t: &RuleTerm| match t {
            RuleTerm::Field(f) => RuleTerm::Field(map(f)),
            _ => t.clone(),
        };
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a, b) => Self::Eq(term(a), term(b)),
            Self::Ne(a, b) => Self::Ne(term(a), term(b)),
            Self::And(xs) => Self::And(xs.iter().map(|r| r.map_fields(map)).collect()),
            Self::Or(xs) => Self::Or(xs.iter().map(|r| r.map_fields(map)).collect()),
            Self::Not(x) => Self::Not(Box::new(x.map_fields(map))),
            Self::Exists {
                table,
                index,
                key,
                provenance,
                equalities,
            } => Self::Exists {
                table: table.clone(),
                index: index.clone(),
                key: key.iter().map(term).collect(),
                provenance: *provenance,
                equalities: equalities
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(f, t)| (f.clone(), term(t)))
                    .collect(),
            },
            _ => self.clone(),
        }
    }
}

impl Schema {
    pub fn children(&self, parent: &str) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&String, &Table, &Through)> {
        self.tables.iter().filter_map(move |(name, t)| {
            t.through
                .as_ref()
                .filter(|c| c.parent == parent)
                .map(|c| (name, t, c))
        })
    }
    /// Stable ancestor order; admission rejects cycles before execution.
    pub fn through_order(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        fn depth(schema: &Schema, name: &str, seen: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> usize {
            if !seen.insert(name.into()) {
                return 0;
            }
            schema
                .table(name)
                .and_then(|t| t.through.as_ref())
                .map_or(0, |t| 1 + depth(schema, &t.parent, seen))
        }
        let mut names: Vec<_> = self.tables.keys().cloned().collect();
        names.sort_by_key(|name| (depth(self, name, &mut BTreeSet::new()), name.clone()));
        names
    }
}

pub fn validate(schema: &Schema) -> Vec<SchemaIssue> {
    let mut issues = Vec::new();
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        let Some(t) = &table.through else {
            continue;
        };
        let mut refuse = |message: String| {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue {
                path: format!("tables.{name}.through"),
                message,
            })
        };
        if table.sync.is_some() && table.leave == LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory && table.columns.contains_key("retained") {
            refuse("read through delivered history reserves retained metadata; rename the column to wasRetained".into());
        }
        let Some(parent) = schema.table(&t.parent) else {
            refuse("read through needs an ordinary application parent table".into());
            continue;
        };
        if schema.maintain_for(&t.parent).is_some()
            || schema.timelines.iter().any(|tl| tl.owns(&t.parent))
            || !schema.visible(&t.parent)
        {
            refuse(
                "read through cannot target engine-owned rows; use an ordinary application parent"
                    .into(),
            );
        }
        if table.columns.get(&t.reference)
            != Some(&ColumnType::Ref {
                table: t.parent.clone(),
            })
            || table.defaults.contains_key(&t.reference)
            || !table.immutable_fields().any(|f| f == t.reference)
        {
            refuse(format!(
                "read through needs required `{0}: {1}` without a default and `immutable {0}`",
                t.reference, t.parent
            ));
        }
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        parent.rules.read.fields(&mut fields);
        let mapped: BTreeSet<_> = t.fields.values().cloned().collect();
        if fields != mapped
            || (parent
                .rules
                .read
                .map_fields(&|f| format!("${}.{f}", t.reference))
                != table.rules.read
                && !(parent.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable()
                    && table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable()))
        {
            refuse("read through must preserve the parent's complete read rule and all captured inputs".into());
        }
        for (capture, field) in &t.fields {
            let kind = if field == "id" {
                Some(ColumnType::Ref {
                    table: t.parent.clone(),
                })
            } else {
                parent.columns.get(field).cloned()
            };
            if capture != &format!("${}.{field}", t.reference)
                || table.columns.get(capture) != kind.as_ref()
                || table.defaults.contains_key(capture)
                || !(field == "id"
                    || parent.engine_field(field)
                    || t.live.is_some() || parent.immutable_fields().any(|f| f == field))
            {
                refuse(format!(
                    "captured {0}.{field} must be immutable and keep its stored type; add `immutable {field}` on {0}",
                    t.parent
                ));
            }
        }
        if t.live.is_none() && parent
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|field| !parent.immutable_fields().any(|f| f == field))
        {
            refuse("the parent's authored deadline must be immutable".into());
        }
        if t.expiring != (table.expire.is_some() || parent.is_expiring())
            || table.is_expiring() && table.expiry_index().is_none()
        {
            refuse("read through needs the inherited expiry classification and effective deadline index".into());
        }
        for field in [PARENT_DEADLINE, DEADLINE] {
            if table.columns.get(field) != Some(&ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(ColumnType::Time)))
                || table.defaults.contains_key(field)
            {
                refuse(format!(
                    "read through needs the engine-owned optional time {field}, without a default"
                ));
            }
        }
        if table.index(CHILDREN).is_none_or(|i| {
            !i.is_plain()
                || i.unique
                || i.columns() != vec![t.reference.clone(), DEADLINE.into(), "id".into()]
        }) {
            refuse("read through needs its physical-child lifetime index".into());
        }
        let mut seen = BTreeSet::from([name.as_str()]);
        let mut p = t.parent.as_str();
        loop {
            if !seen.insert(p) {
                refuse("read through cycle; break the cycle with an ordinary read rule".into());
                break;
            }
            match schema.table(p).and_then(|t| t.through.as_ref()) {
                Some(t) => p = &t.parent,
                None => break,
            }
        }
    }
    issues
}
//! Row-local contributions shared by native and wasm writes, expiry and adoption.
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Maintain, Table};
use crate::value::Value;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CalendarKey {
    pub function: String,
    pub column: String,
    pub millis: i64,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "zero")]
    pub offset: i64,
}

impl CalendarKey {
    pub fn value(&self, row: &Value) -> Value {
        if self.column.contains('.') && !matches!(row.field(&self.column), Ok(Value::Int(_))) { return Value::Null; }
        let at = row.field(&self.column).ok().and_then(|v| v.as_int().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        Value::Int(match self.function.as_str() {
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#23-index-keys-views-and-windows — civil keys carry no duration; the same function as the builtin.
            "month" => crate::calendar::month_of(at),
            "year" => crate::calendar::year_of(at),
            _ => calendar_ordinal(at, self.millis, self.offset),
        })
    }

    /// `month` and `year` count civil periods; the others count fixed durations.
    pub fn civil(&self) -> bool { matches!(self.function.as_str(), "month" | "year") }
    pub fn validate(&self, source: &Table) -> Result<(), String> {
        if !source.columns.get(&self.column).is_some_and(|kind| kind == &ColumnType::Time
            || (source.recorded.contains_key(&self.column) && kind.required() == &ColumnType::Time))
            || (self.civil() && (self.millis != 0 || self.offset != 0))
            || (!self.civil() && (self.millis <= 0 || self.offset < 0 || self.offset >= self.millis))
            || !matches!(self.function.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket" | "month" | "year")
            || (self.function == "day" && self.millis != 86_400_000) || (self.function == "hour" && self.millis != 3_600_000) {
            return Err("computed time keys need a required time column; day, hour and bucket take a positive period and a nonnegative offset smaller than it, month and year take neither".into());
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn zero(value: &i64) -> bool { *value == 0 }

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — anchored UTC ordinals use the existing integer key encoding on every host; zero offsets retain the old serialized form.
pub fn calendar_ordinal(at: i64, millis: i64, offset: i64) -> i64 {
    ((i128::from(at) - i128::from(offset)).div_euclid(i128::from(millis))) as i64
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Operand { Field(String), Literal(Value) }
impl Operand {
    fn value<'a>(&'a self, row: &'a Value, member: Option<&'a Value>) -> &'a Value {
        match self {
            Self::Field(field) => match member.zip(field.strip_prefix("$row.")) {
                Some((member, field)) => member.field(field),
                None => row.field(field),
            }.unwrap_or(&Value::Null),
            Self::Literal(value) => value,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Predicate {
    Bool(bool), Field(String), Not(Box<Predicate>), And(Box<Predicate>, Box<Predicate>), Or(Box<Predicate>, Box<Predicate>),
    Compare { op: String, left: Operand, right: Operand },
}
impl Predicate {
    pub fn matches(&self, row: &Value) -> bool {
        self.matches_with_member(row, None)
    }

    // One boolean/comparison walker; only comparison operands can refer to a
    // keyed member. Bare boolean fields keep their source-row interpretation.
    fn matches_with_member(&self, row: &Value, member: Option<&Value>) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Bool(value) => *value,
            Self::Field(field) => matches!(row.field(field), Ok(Value::Bool(true))),
            Self::Not(value) => !value.matches_with_member(row, member),
            Self::And(a, b) => a.matches_with_member(row, member) && b.matches_with_member(row, member),
            Self::Or(a, b) => a.matches_with_member(row, member) || b.matches_with_member(row, member),
            Self::Compare { op, left, right } => {
                let (a, b) = (left.value(row, member), right.value(row, member));
                match op.as_str() { "=" => a == b, "!=" => a != b, "<" => a.total_cmp(b).is_lt(), "<=" => !a.total_cmp(b).is_gt(), ">" => a.total_cmp(b).is_gt(), ">=" => !a.total_cmp(b).is_lt(), _ => false }
            }
        }
    }
    pub fn steps(&self) -> u64 {
        match self { Self::Not(p) => 1 + p.steps(), Self::And(a,b) | Self::Or(a,b) => 1 + a.steps() + b.steps(), Self::Compare { .. } => 3, _ => 1 }
    }
    #[inline(never)] // Keep one owned-field collector around the borrowed traversal.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        self.each_field(&mut |field| { out.insert(field.to_owned()); });
    }
    /// Borrow fields for dependency observation without constructing a copied set.
    pub(crate) fn each_field<'a>(&'a self, out: &mut dyn FnMut(&'a str)) {
        match self {
            Self::Field(field) => out(field),
            Self::Not(p) => p.each_field(out), Self::And(a,b) | Self::Or(a,b) => { a.each_field(out); b.each_field(out); }
            Self::Compare { left, right, .. } => {
                if let Operand::Field(field) = left { out(field); }
                if let Operand::Field(field) = right { out(field); }
            }
            Self::Bool(_) => {}
        }
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — one evaluator supplies filters and Euclidean calendar keys on every host and lifecycle path.
impl Maintain {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — expired recipients can miss deltas and revive on clock rollback.
    // Effective member expiry includes read-through ancestors, even over a timeless source.
    pub fn row_count_needs_live_basis(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema) -> bool {
        self.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|key|
            schema.tables[&self.source].is_expiring() || schema.tables[&key.table].is_expiring())
    }

    pub fn selected(&self, image: &Value) -> bool { if self.private.is_some() && self.by.iter().any(|f| matches!(image.field(f), Err(_) | Ok(Value::Null))) { return false; } self.filter.as_ref().is_none_or(|p| p.matches(image)) }
    pub fn source_key(&self, image: &Value) -> Vec<Value> {
        self.by.iter().map(|field| match self.computed.get(field) {
            Some(key) => key.value(image),
            None => image.field(self.aliases.get(field).unwrap_or(field)).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null),
        }).collect()
    }
    pub fn bare_key(&self, field: &str) -> bool { self.by.iter().any(|f| f == field) && !self.computed.contains_key(field) }
    pub fn evaluation_steps(&self) -> u64 { self.filter.as_ref().map_or(0, Predicate::steps) + self.by.len() as u64 + self.computed.len() as u64 + self.computed.values().filter(|key| key.offset != 0).count() as u64 }
    pub fn dependencies(&self) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        if let Some(k)=&self.row_key { k.predicate.fields(&mut fields); fields.retain(|f|!f.starts_with("$row.")); return fields; }
        if let Some(p) = &self.filter { p.fields(&mut fields); }
        for field in &self.by { fields.insert(self.computed.get(field).map_or(self.aliases.get(field).unwrap_or(field), |key| &key.column).clone()); }
        if let crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { of } = &self.kind { fields.insert(of.clone()); }
        fields
    }
    pub fn contribution_index(&self) -> String { format!("$contribution:{}", self.target) }
    pub fn source_scan<'a>(&self, source: &'a Table, group: &[Value]) -> (&'a str, Vec<Value>) {
        let name = self.contribution_index();
        if let Some((name, _)) = source.indexes.get_key_value(&name) { return (name, group.to_vec()); }
        source.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| index.is_plain() && index.columns().starts_with(&self.by))
            .map(|(name, _)| (name.as_str(), group.to_vec())).unwrap_or(("byId", Vec::new()))
    }
    /// The group also determines the engine's synthetic id, never its value.
    pub fn read_uses_non_key_fields(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema) -> bool {
        let Some(target) = schema.table(&self.target) else { return false; };
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        target.rules.read.fields(&mut fields);
        fields.iter().any(|field| field != "id" && !self.group_fields().contains(field))
    }

    pub fn expiring_read_refusal(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema) -> Option<String> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — a row-keyed count is authorized by its member row and certifies its live basis.
        if self.row_key.is_some() { return None; }
        (schema.table(&self.source).is_some_and(Table::is_expiring) && self.read_uses_non_key_fields(schema)).then(||
            format!("{}: expiring aggregate authorization must use only group keys; use a rule over {}, omit read to inherit the source rule, or use online only with an explicit key rule; a cap cannot authorize the live value before recounting", self.target, self.group_fields().join(", ")))
    }

    pub fn groups_by_parent(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema) -> bool {
        schema.table(&self.source).and_then(|source| source.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|parent| self.by.contains(&parent.reference))
    }

pub(super) struct RuleProbe<'a, 'b> {
    pub(super) runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>,
    pub(super) allowance: &'a Authorization,
}
impl Probe for RuleProbe<'_, '_> {
    fn reference(&mut self, table: &str, id: &Value, field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> { self.runner.rule_reference(table, id, field) }
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.runner.rule_probe_exists(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities)
    }
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        self.runner.created(table, index, key)
    }
}

/// The scanned prefix as the row a rule is judged against: each pinned column with its value.
fn prefix_row(index: &Index, prefix: &[Value]) -> Value {
    Value::Row(index.components.iter().zip(prefix).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name.clone(), value.clone()))).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>().into())
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — the dependency envelope is schema-derived too; short-circuit probes cannot disclose a hidden answer through tables.
    pub(super) fn rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table).and_then(|m|m.row_key.as_ref()) {
                self.read_tables.insert(key.table.clone());
            }
            self.aggregate_source_dependency(&table);
            self.read_tables.insert(table);
        }
    }

    fn metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .filter(|maintain| self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring()) {
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn metered_rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let site_heap = rule_probe_sites_heap(rule);
        let site_mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(site_heap)?;
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(row_key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table)
                .and_then(|maintain| maintain.row_key.as_ref()) {
                metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &row_key.table)?;
            }
            self.metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&table)?;
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &table)?;
        }
        self.meter.release_row_heap(site_mark, site_heap);
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — trusted delivery avoids physical probes,
    // not the logical prefix verdict or its scratch admission.
    fn reserve_delivered_scan(&mut self, table: &Table, visibility: rules::Visibility, admitted: bool, query: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && (query || self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort) && !self.context.is_system()
            // An admitted merge lane already paid its guard/discovery proof.
            // Reserve exactly the native lane price, including an empty range.
            && self.merge_grant.is_none()
            && admitted && visibility.uniform && !visibility.decided_true && !table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() {
            let allowance = [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))];
            if self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort { self.authorized_scan(&allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            else { self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            return Ok(true);
        }
        Ok(false)
    }

//! Narrow stable leading-require reduction for operation-specific authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#81-exact-conservative-boundary-review-clarification — unknown write sets never establish stability.
use super::*;
use crate::ast::{Stmt,ProgramDecl,ProgramKind,InvariantDecl,RuleDecl};
use super::super::catalog::TableInfo;
pub(super) fn resolve<'a>(catalog:&'a Catalog,decl:&InvariantDecl)->Option<(String,&'a ProgramDecl)>{
    if matches!(decl.target.as_str(),"read"|"insert"|"update"|"delete"|"always"|"write"){return None;}
    let matches=catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|{let module=std::path::Path::new(&d.file).file_stem().unwrap().to_string_lossy();d.programs.iter().map(move|p|(super::super::operation_name(&module,&p.name),p))})
        .filter(|(name,p)|p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&(name==&decl.target||p.name==decl.target)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if matches.len()==1 {matches.into_iter().next()}else{None}
}
fn safe(rule:&RuleExpr)->bool {match rule{
    RuleExpr::Exists{key,equalities,..}=>key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).all(|t|matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))),
    RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().all(safe),
    RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>[a,b].iter().all(|t|matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))),
    RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Deny|RuleExpr::Public{..}=>true,_=>false,
}}
pub(super) fn requirements(catalog:&Catalog,info:&TableInfo,p:&ProgramDecl)->Vec<RuleExpr>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for s in p.prelude.iter().chain(&p.body){let Stmt::Require{condition,site,..}=s else{break};
        let decl=RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value:condition.clone(),site:site.clone()};
        if let Ok(rule)=super::super::schema::bind_rule(catalog,info,&decl)&&safe(&rule){out.push(rule);}
    }out
}
pub(super) fn prove(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->Result<bool,String>{
    let p=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|Some(&p.program.name)==inv.operation.as_ref()).ok_or("operation did not bind")?;
    if !safe(inv.policy.as_ref().ok_or("unsupported policy")?){return Err("operation policy needs unsupported target-image, negative-probe or live-reference bindings".into());}
    let writes=snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program);
    if !writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table){return Err("operation has no direct write to the named table; implicit effects require a reduction".into());}
    let dependencies=inv.requirements.iter().flat_map(kernel::dependencies).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    if writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|dependencies.contains(t)) {return Err("require dependency can be changed by this operation, including its target write".into());}
    if bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|!t.maintained.is_empty()||!t.sequences.is_empty()||t.on_delete.values().any(|a|a.writes())) || !bound.schema.maintains.is_empty(){return Err("implicit maintain/sequence/referential writes require dependency stability reduction".into());}
    // Source helpers/control flow may hide write sets or viewer changes.
    let json=serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap();
    fn opaque(v:&serde_json::Value)->bool{match v{serde_json::Value::Object(m)=>m.keys().any(|k|matches!(k.as_str(),"For"|"If"|"JobAs"|"JobQuery"|"Emit"))||m.values().any(opaque),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(opaque),_=>false}}
    if opaque(&json){return Err("operation control flow or opaque effects are outside stable leading-require reduction".into());}
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(inv.requirements.clone());
    // Table-rule gates can only further restrict a dominating stable require;
    // using the require alone overapproximates effective owner-field gates.
    kernel::implies(&admission,inv.policy.as_ref().unwrap())
}
//! The binder's type model: what a Quarry expression is, statically, so a
//! program's arguments and result reach the client as TypeScript types by
//! inference (nothing is annotated, nothing is committed).

use crate::ast::{ScalarType, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, TextFormat};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Ty {
    Unknown,
    Null,
    Bool,
    Int,
    Time,
    Decimal(u8),
    Money(String, u8),
    Text(u32),
    Id,
    Principal,
    Ref(String),
    Bytes,
    Json,
    Image,
    Video,
    File,
    Enum(Vec<String>),
    Cursor,
    Optional(Box<Ty>),
    /// A bounded list of scalars; `max` bounds a loop over it.
    List(Box<Ty>, u32),
    /// One row of a table, present.
    Row(String),
    WriteRow(String),
    /// A bounded page of rows, in index order.
    Page(String, u32),
    /// A shaped page: at most `max` objects.
    Collection(Box<Ty>, u32),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// A projection whose row base carries optional device metadata.
    RowShape(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// Object-spread fields with row metadata, but ordinary object wildcard order.
    RowObject(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),

}

impl Ty {
    /// A reassigned allocation is bounded by the heap meter, not its first literal's length.
    pub(crate) fn rebound(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Text(_) => Ty::Text(u32::MAX),
            // A known empty value stays empty, even at a loop header.
            Ty::List(_, 0) | Ty::Collection(_, 0) => self,
            Ty::List(item, _) => Ty::List(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Collection(item, _) => Ty::Collection(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Object(fields) => Ty::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowShape(fields) => Ty::RowShape(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowObject(fields) => Ty::RowObject(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.rebound().optional(),
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn optional(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null | Ty::Unknown => self,
            other => Ty::Optional(Box::new(other)),
        }
    }

    pub fn required(&self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref().clone(),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null)
    }

    pub fn is_bool(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Bool)
    }

    pub fn is_numeric(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Int | Ty::Time | Ty::Decimal(_) | Ty::Money(..))
    }

    pub fn is_identity(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Id | Ty::Principal | Ty::Ref(_))
    }

    pub fn from_scalar(scalar: &ScalarType) -> Ty {
        let base = match &scalar.kind {
            TypeKind::Cursor => Ty::Cursor,
            TypeKind::Text { max, .. } => Ty::Text(*max),
            TypeKind::Id => Ty::Id,
            TypeKind::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            TypeKind::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            TypeKind::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            TypeKind::Time => Ty::Time,
            TypeKind::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            TypeKind::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            TypeKind::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            TypeKind::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            TypeKind::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            TypeKind::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            TypeKind::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            TypeKind::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            TypeKind::List { item, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_scalar(item)), *max),
        };
        if scalar.optional { base.optional() } else { base }
    }

    pub fn from_column(kind: &ColumnType) -> Ty {
        match kind {
            ColumnType::Id => Ty::Id,
            ColumnType::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            ColumnType::Text { max } => Ty::Text(*max),
            ColumnType::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            ColumnType::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            ColumnType::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            ColumnType::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            ColumnType::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            ColumnType::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            ColumnType::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            ColumnType::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            ColumnType::Time => Ty::Time,
            ColumnType::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            ColumnType::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            ColumnType::Optional(inner) => Ty::from_column(inner).optional(),
            ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_column(of)), *max),
        }
    }

    /// The name a diagnostic prints.

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Maintain {
    /// Compiler-owned whole owner-key partition (LLP 3000.028 §3.4).
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub own_keys: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub row_key: Option<crate::maintain::RowKey>,
    pub parent_key: Option<through::Through>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "BTreeMap::is_empty")]
    pub aliases: BTreeMap<String,String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub private: Option<columns::ColumnOwner>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub filter: Option<crate::maintain::Predicate>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "BTreeMap::is_empty")]
    pub computed: BTreeMap<String, crate::maintain::CalendarKey>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Residency::is_default")]
    pub residency: Residency,
    pub kind: MaintainKind,
    pub source: String,
    pub by: Vec<String>,
    pub target: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub readable: Option<RuleExpr>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum MaintainKind {
    Count,
//! Saved-source fixture selection, business cases and the query sweep.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#9-wave-13-saved-version-proofs-day3-2026-10-16 — contemporary fixtures belong to the selected saved source.
use super::*;

const CASE_SUMMARY: &str = "SNAPBACK4_TEST_CASES";

fn case_count(path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<usize, String> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
    Ok(text.lines().filter(|line| {
        let line = line.trim();
        !line.is_empty() && !line.starts_with("//")
    }).count())
}

/// `test`: run every argument-free query as each persona against the store
/// (optionally loaded from a fixture) and print the meters beside the
/// ceilings, so a builder reads numbers instead of being refused on a bound.
pub fn test(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    let project = match proof_source::project(options, "source") { Ok(project) => project, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let sources = match proof_source::sources(&project, options, "source") { Ok(sources) => sources, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let bound = compiled_or_exit(proof_source::compile(&sources, options, "source"), options.json);
    let mut defaults = options.clone();
    let mut skipped_business_cases = 0;
    if !defaults.flags.contains_key("fixture") {
        let saved = options.flags.get("source").map(std::path::Path::new).map(|path| {
            if path.is_dir() { path.join("fixtures") }
            else if path.file_name().is_some_and(|name| name == "schema.q") { path.parent().unwrap().join("fixtures") }
            else { path.with_file_name(format!("{}-fixtures", path.file_stem().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy())) }
        });
        if let Some(fixture) = saved.filter(|path| path.is_dir()) {
            eprintln!("test: using {} (saved version's contemporary fixtures; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display());
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir() {
            let fixture = project.root.join("fixtures");
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                defaults.flags.insert("saved-fixture-fallback".into(), String::new());
                eprintln!("test: no contemporary fixtures for saved source; validating only declared-table files in {}; pass --fixture <dir> for a complete contemporary case suite", fixture.display());
                let cases = fixture.join(test_cases::FILE);
                if cases.is_file() {
                    skipped_business_cases = match case_count(&cases) { Ok(count) => count, Err(error) => { eprintln!("test: {error}"); return 1; } };
                    eprintln!("test: skipped {} — working cases may target another release; pass --fixture <dir> to run explicitly", cases.display());
                }
            } else { eprintln!("test: using {} (default fixture directory; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display()); }
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            eprintln!("test: no saved or working fixtures; query sweep only; pass --fixture <dir> for business cases");
        }
    }
    let options = &defaults;
    // test applies dev's cast: a persona dev would refuse is not run as an ordinary viewer.
    // Residency measures any principal in the data (scale fixtures name thousands), so it notes instead.
    if let Some(why) = options.flags.get("as").and_then(|names| names.split(',').find_map(|name| test_cases::undeclared_persona(name, &bound.personas))) {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
            eprintln!("test --as: {why}");
            return 1;
        }
        eprintln!("test --residency --as: measuring an undeclared principal; dev would refuse it as a persona");
    }
    let _owner = if options.flags.contains_key("fixture") || options.flags.contains_key("source") { None } else {
        match crate::store_owner::command(&project.store_path()) {
            Ok(owner) => Some(owner),
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    let fixture_now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let opened = match &_owner {
        Some(owner) => owner.open_engine(&bound, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), fixture_now).map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
        None => test_fixture::engine_at(&project, &bound, options, fixture_now),
    };
    let mut engine = match opened {
        Ok(engine) => engine,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
    };
    let personas = residency::personas(&bound, options);
    let sweep_personas = if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") { Default::default() } else {
        match test_sweep::load(options.flags.get("fixture"), &bound) {
            Ok(matrix) => matrix,
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    if let Some(persona) = options.flags.get("as") && !sweep_personas.is_empty() {
        println!("test --as {persona} overrides test.sweep.json because --as requests one deliberate global probe; for: {}", sweep_personas.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
        let verbose = options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
        if let Err(error) = residency::measure(&mut engine, &bound, &personas, verbose) { eprintln!("residency measurement failed: {error}"); return 1; }
        if !verbose {
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                println!("{CASE_SUMMARY} {{\"run\":0,\"skipped\":{skipped_business_cases}}}");
            }
            if !options.flags.contains_key("fixture-only") {
                eprintln!("test --residency executed no business cases; use --fixture-only for acquisition measurement, or omit --residency to run the cases");
                return 1;
            }
            return 0;
        }
    }
    let mut security_campaign = snapback4_lang::security::challenges::run(&bound);
    if options.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
        match test_fixture::engine_at(&project,&bound,options,snapback4_lang::security::challenges::NOW){
            Ok(security_fixture)=>snapback4_lang::security::challenges::operations(&bound,&security_fixture,&mut security_campaign),
            Err(why)=>{security_campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("trusted fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
        }
    }else{
        security_campaign.unsupported.insert("no trusted fixture files for the fixed campaign clock; development store is not replayed".into());
        if let Ok(store)=snapback4_core::Store::memory(){
            match snapback4_core::Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),snapback4_lang::security::challenges::NOW){
                Ok(isolated)=>snapback4_lang::security::challenges::operations(&bound,&isolated,&mut security_campaign),
                Err(why)=>{security_campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("schema matrix unavailable: {why}"));}
            }
//! The same fixture loader serves query testing and residency at either CLI entry point.
use super::Options;
use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_core::{Engine, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;

pub(super) fn engine(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    engine_at(project, bound, options, snapback4_core::now_ms())
}

pub(super) fn engine_at(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    let Some(fixture) = options.flags.get("fixture") else {
        if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            return Engine::new_at(store, bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|e| e.to_string());
        }
        return project.open_engine(bound).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open the store: {error}"));
    };
    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut schema = bound.schema.clone();
    // Residency defers ordinary maintains until after bulk loading and builds
    // them once; column derives stay installed and maintain as rows insert.
    let residency = options.flags.contains_key("residency");
    if residency { schema.maintains.retain(|m| m.private.is_some()); }
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut engine = Engine::new_at(store, schema, bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(fixture).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))? {
        let entry = entry.map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))?;
        names.push(entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string());
    }
    names.sort();
    // One batch for the whole directory: the engine orders parents before
    // children within a batch, so file order (and read_dir order) cannot make
    // a read-through child or a reference miss its parent.
    let mut rows = Vec::new();
    let mut unknown = Vec::new();
    for name in names {
        let Some(table) = name.strip_suffix(".jsonl") else { continue };
        if name == super::test_cases::FILE { continue; }
        let path = std::path::Path::new(fixture).join(&name);
        let Some(definition) = bound.schema.tables.get(table) else {
            if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") {
                eprintln!("test: skipped {} — saved source declares no table {table}; pass --fixture <dir> to select contemporary fixtures explicitly", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — the starter's own unmodified sample is skipped loudly; a project's file for a missing table still refuses.
            if !options.flags.contains_key("source") && super::init::starter_fixture(&name).is_some_and(|starter| std::fs::read_to_string(&path).is_ok_and(|text| text == starter)) {
                eprintln!("test: WARNING: skipped {} — the todo starter's unmodified sample rows for a `{table}` table your snapback/*.q does not declare; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's other leftovers", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            unknown.push(name);
            continue;
        };
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture file {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
        for (line_number, line) in text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty()) {
            let at = format!("{name}:{}", line_number + 1);
            let mut json: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
            // A residency fixture is often an exported store: engine-maintained
            // columns are dropped and rebuilt from the source rows. `test`
            // refuses them as an insert and `/seed` do (E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN).
            if residency && let Some(fields) = json.as_object_mut() { fields.retain(|field, _| !definition.maintained.contains_key(field)); }
            relative_times(definition, &mut json, now).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
            if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &json) { return Err(format!("{at}: {why}")); }
            let row = wire::row_from_json(definition, &json).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}\n  hint: a fixture row is one JSON object per line with the table's fields and an \"id\"; see snapback4 guide testing"))?;
            rows.push((table.to_owned(), row));
        }
    }
    // A misspelled file name would otherwise load nothing and still pass.
    if !unknown.is_empty() {
        let tables: Vec<&str> = bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        // 0.4.17's init wrote the todo starter's rows beside a project's own schema.
        let starter = unknown.iter().any(|name| name == "todos.jsonl")
            && std::fs::read_to_string(std::path::Path::new(fixture).join("todos.jsonl")).is_ok_and(|text| text.contains("\"id\":\"todo-milk\""));
        let starter = if starter { format!("\n  hint: todos.jsonl is the todo starter's sample that `snapback4 init` wrote; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's {} while they are unmodified (or delete them, and the starter's list/add/toggle/remove lines, yourself)", super::test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() };
        return Err(format!("{fixture}: {} name{} no table in snapback/*.q; nothing was run{starter}\n  hint: a fixture file is <table>.jsonl for a declared table ({}), plus {}; see snapback4 guide testing",
            unknown.join(", "), if unknown.len() == 1 { "s" } else { "" }, tables.join(", "), super::test_cases::FILE));
    }
    // Fixture rows take `/seed`'s insert path (defaults, sequences, unique with the author's codes, caps, maintained views), not the trusted bulk import.
    let loaded = engine.seed_at(rows, now).map_err(|error| {
        let text = error.to_string();
        let hint = if text.contains("NOT_FOUND") { "\n  hint: every fixture file loads as one batch, parents first; NOT_FOUND means a referenced or read-through parent row is in no fixture file (check the id)" } else { "" };
        format!("{fixture}: {text}{hint}")
    })?;
    if residency {
        engine.adopt_at(bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot build fixture maintains: {error}"))?;
    }
    if !options.json { println!("test: loaded {loaded} rows from {fixture} in {:.1} s", started.elapsed().as_secs_f64()); }
    Ok(engine)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#5-fixtures-and-dev-evidence — relative time leaves, seeds and operations share one captured clock.
/// Only typed time leaves interpret `now`: text and JSON retain their literals.
pub(crate) fn relative_times(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, row: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = row.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = table.columns.get(name) { time_value(kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?; }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub(super) fn time_value(kind: &snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType, value: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), value) if !value.is_null() => time_value(inner, value, now),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, serde_json::Value::Array(items)) => {
            for item in items { time_value(of, item, now)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        (ColumnType::Time, value) => {
            let Some(text) = value.as_str().filter(|text| text.starts_with("now")) else { return Ok(()) };
            let delta = if text == "now" { 0 } else {
                let offset = text.strip_prefix("now").unwrap();
                if !offset.starts_with(['+', '-']) { return Err(format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: expected now or now±duration (ms, s, m, h, d, w)")); }
                crate::dev_clock::offset(offset).map_err(|why| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: {}", why.message))?
            };
            let resolved = now.checked_add(delta).filter(|time| (0..=snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX).contains(time))
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?} is outside Unix milliseconds 0..9007199254740991"))?;
            *value = resolved.into();
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}
pub(super) fn argument_times(kinds: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = args.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = kinds.get(name).and_then(snapback4_core::schema::arg_as_column) {
                time_value(&kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?;
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod column_tests {
    use super::*;
    use snapback4_core::{Context, Value};
    #[test]
    fn residency_fixture_import_rebuilds_poisoned_columns() {
        let source=include_str!("../../../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/maintained-columns.q");
        let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty());
        let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("f1a-fixture-{}",std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        {
            let files=[("posts",r#"{"id":"p","at":1,"likeCount":999,"selected":999,"total":999}"#),
                ("likes",r#"{"id":"l","postId":"p","active":true,"amount":7}"#)];
            for index in [0,1] {std::fs::write(root.join(format!("{}.jsonl",files[index].0)),files[index].1).unwrap();}
            let options=Options {positionals:vec![],json:true,flags:[("fixture".into(),root.to_str().unwrap().into()),("residency".into(),String::new())].into()};
            let project=Project {root:root.clone()};
            let mut engine=engine(&project,&bound,&options).unwrap();
            let row=engine.query("postRow",Context::new("alice",[("id".into(),Value::Id("p".into()))].into()).at(1000)).unwrap().data;
            assert_eq!(["likeCount","selected","total"].map(|field|row.field(field).unwrap().as_int().unwrap()),[1,1,7]);
            assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("posts").unwrap(),vec![row]);
            for m in &engine.schema().maintains {
                let rows=engine.store().rows(&m.target).unwrap();
                assert_eq!(rows.len(),1);
                assert_eq!(rows[0].field("value").unwrap(),&Value::Int(if m.private.as_ref().unwrap().column=="total" {7} else {1}));
            }
            for (table,_) in files {std::fs::remove_file(root.join(format!("{table}.jsonl"))).unwrap();}
        }
        std::fs::remove_dir(&root).unwrap();
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod order_tests {
    use super::*;
    // `items` sorts before `lists`: the child's file is read first, yet the
    // directory loads as one batch with parents ordered before children.
    #[test]
    fn read_through_child_file_before_parent_file_loads() {
        let source = "use personas alice, bob\n\ntable lists:\n  owner: principal\n  title: text 1..80\n  immutable owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  cap 50 by owner\n  sync to .owner\n\ntable items:\n  parent: lists\n  text: text 1..200\n  immutable parent\n  read through .parent\n  insert <- .parent.owner = viewer\n  cap 50 by parent\n";
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("fixture-order-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("items.jsonl"), "{\"id\":\"00000000000000000000000C01\",\"parent\":\"00000000000000000000000B01\",\"text\":\"wash\"}\n").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("lists.jsonl"), "{\"id\":\"00000000000000000000000B01\",\"owner\":\"dev:alice\",\"title\":\"Home\"}\n").unwrap();
        let options = Options { positionals: vec![], json: true, flags: [("fixture".into(), root.to_str().unwrap().into())].into() };
        let project = Project { root: root.clone() };
        let engine = engine(&project, &bound, &options);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        let engine = engine.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("items").unwrap().len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("lists").unwrap().len(), 1);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod relative_tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;
    #[test]
    fn relative_time_is_typed_checked_and_does_not_rewrite_text_or_json() {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table notes:\n  at: time\n  optionalAt: time ?\n  title: text <=80\n  payload: json <=200\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut table = bound.schema.tables["notes"].clone();
        use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
        table.columns.insert("times".into(), ColumnType::List { of: Box::new(ColumnType::Optional(Box::new(ColumnType::Time))), max: 4 });
        let now = 1_000_000_000;
        for (text, expected) in [("now", now), ("now-1h", now-3_600_000), ("now+2d", now+172_800_000), ("now+1ms", now+1)] {
            let mut row = json!({"at":text, "optionalAt":text, "title":"now-1h", "payload":{"time":"now"}});
            relative_times(&table, &mut row, now).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(row, json!({"at":expected,"optionalAt":expected,"title":"now-1h","payload":{"time":"now"}}));
        }
        for text in ["nowsoon", "now+0h", "now+1.5h", "now-9999999999999999999h", "now-100w"] {
            let error = relative_times(&table, &mut json!({"at":text}), now).unwrap_err();
            assert!(error.contains("E_FIXTURE_TIME") && error.contains(text), "{error}");
        }
        let mut null = json!({"at":42,"optionalAt":null});
        relative_times(&table, &mut null, now).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(null, json!({"at":42,"optionalAt":null}));
        let mut list = json!({"times":["now", null, "now-1s"]});
        relative_times(&table, &mut list, now).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(list, json!({"times":[now, null, now-1000]}));
    }
    #[test]
    fn seed_defaults_and_relative_rows_share_the_captured_clock() {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table notes:\n  at: time default now\n  ends: time\n  expire at .ends\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\nquery one():\n  return notes[\"n\"]\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("rel3-relative-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("notes.jsonl"), r#"{"id":"n","ends":"now+1ms"}"#).unwrap();
        let options = Options { positionals: vec![], json: true, flags: [("fixture".into(),root.to_str().unwrap().into())].into() };
        let now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let mut engine = engine_at(&Project { root:root.clone() }, &bound, &options, now).unwrap();
        let row = engine.query("one", snapback4_core::Context::new("dev:alice", Default::default()).at(now)).unwrap().data;
        assert_eq!(row.field("at").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Int(now));
        assert_eq!(row.field("ends").unwrap(), &snapback4_core::Value::Int(now+1));
        let gone = engine.query("one", snapback4_core::Context::new("dev:alice", Default::default()).at(now+1)).unwrap().data;
        assert_eq!(gone, snapback4_core::Value::Null);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
    }
}

use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}

//! Logical observer obligations, separate from the whole declaration verdict.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#101-separate-observer-obligations — pull group authority back to each contributor.
use super::*;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Obligation {
    pub path: String,
    pub status: Status,
    pub reason: String,
    pub repair: Option<String>,
}
impl Obligation {
    fn implication(path: String, admission: Result<RuleExpr, String>, policy: &RuleExpr) -> Self {
        let result = admission.and_then(|a| kernel::implies(&a, policy));
        let (status, reason, repair) = match result {
            Ok(true) => (Status::Proved, "every admitted logical contributor satisfies this policy".into(), None),
            Ok(false) => (Status::Unproved, "observer authority does not imply the source policy".into(),
                Some("retain the privacy fields as bare group keys and apply the source privacy policy to this observer".into())),
            Err(why) => (Status::Unproved, why, Some("use ordinary group keys and a read rule over those keys; keep opaque/temporal derivations private".into())),
        };
        Self { path, status, reason, repair }
    }
}

pub(super) fn obligations(bound: &Bound, inv: &Invariant) -> Vec<Obligation> {
    if inv.target != "read" { return Vec::new(); }
    let Some(policy) = &inv.policy else { return Vec::new(); };
    let table = &bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
    let mut out = vec![Obligation::implication(
        "server point/scan/planned view/count/sum/exists payload".into(),
        Ok(super::columns::admission(table,inv.column.as_deref())), policy)];
    for m in bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m| m.source == inv.table) {
        let admission = (|| {
            if m.row_key.is_some() || m.parent_key.is_some() || m.private.is_some()
                || !m.aliases.is_empty() || !m.computed.is_empty() || m.own_keys.is_some()
                || table.read_lifetime_sensitive()
            { return Err(format!("{} -> {}: captured/private/computed/temporal maintenance needs a contributor reduction", m.source, m.target)); }
            let target = bound.schema.table(&m.target).ok_or_else(|| format!("{}: maintained-column observer needs its owning table/field authority", m.target))?;
            if m.by.iter().any(|key|key.contains('.')||key.starts_with('$')||table.recorded.contains_key(key)
                || table.columns.get(key).is_none() || table.columns.get(key)!=target.columns.get(key)) {
                return Err(format!("{} -> {}: group keys are not bare identically typed source/target columns",m.source,m.target));
            }
            if target.through.is_some() || target.read_lifetime_sensitive() {
                return Err(format!("{}: derived observer has inherited/temporal authority", m.target));
            }
            // A target group field equals that field on every contributing
            // source row. Its id/value and all lost fields have no such law.
            pullback(&target.rules.read, &m.by)
        })();
        out.push(Obligation::implication(format!("maintained {:?}: {} -> {}", m.kind, m.source, m.target), admission, policy));
        for next in bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|n| n.source == m.target) {
            out.push(Obligation { path: format!("derived chain {} -> {} -> {}", inv.table, m.target, next.target),
                status: Status::Unproved, reason: "downstream contributor substitution is not implemented".into(),
                repair: Some("query the directly protected aggregate, or declare and verify privacy on each derived observer".into()) });
        }
    }
    out
}

fn pullback(rule: &RuleExpr, keys: &[String]) -> Result<RuleExpr, String> {
    fn term(t: &RuleTerm, keys: &[String]) -> Result<RuleTerm, String> {
        match t {
            RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => Ok(t.clone()),
            RuleTerm::Field(f) if keys.contains(f) => Ok(t.clone()),
            _ => Err("derived rule reads a value/id/lost group field or a reference without a contributor identity".into()),
        }
    }
    Ok(match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public {..} => rule.clone(),
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) => RuleExpr::Eq(term(a,keys)?,term(b,keys)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) => RuleExpr::Ne(term(a,keys)?,term(b,keys)?),
        RuleExpr::Compare(op,a,b) => RuleExpr::Compare(*op,term(a,keys)?,term(b,keys)?),
        RuleExpr::And(xs) => RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|pullback(r,keys)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        RuleExpr::Or(xs) => RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|pullback(r,keys)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        RuleExpr::Not(x) => RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(pullback(x,keys)?)),
        RuleExpr::Exists {table,index,key,provenance,equalities} => RuleExpr::Exists {table:table.clone(),index:index.clone(),
            key:key.iter().map(|t|term(t,keys)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?,provenance:*provenance,
            equalities:equalities.iter().map(|(n,t)|Ok((n.clone(),term(t,keys)?))).collect::<Result<_,String>>()?},
        RuleExpr::Created {..} => return Err("created probe has no derived read contributor reduction".into()),
    })
}
